[1] The Hacker News - pCloud Password Manager Hit by Cyber Attack Involving Email Address Exposure and Login Attempts
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Sat, 28 Nov 2026 13:38:11 +0530
Link: https://thehackernews.com/2026/11/pcloud-password-manager-hit-by-cyber.html

[TAM METİN - 714 kelime]
Cross-platform password manager pCloud Pass has confirmed it was targeted by a cyber attack after users reported seeing unknown devices being connected to their accounts.

"A cyber attack targeting pCloud Pass occurred, which was promptly contained by the pCloud team," the Swiss company said in response to a number of posts on Reddit. "Unfortunately, a small number of pCloud Pass user email addresses were exposed and login attempts were made. Our system sends a notification automatically to each of these addresses."

pCloud emphasized that user data was not compromised in the attack, adding passwords, notes, and other information stored in pCloud Pass are encrypted and can only be accessed after providing a Master Password.

It also said the incident was limited to pCloud Pass, and that pCloud Cloud Storage services are not affected.

"With the advancement of AI, vulnerabilities in the software that every online service relies upon (such as operating systems, programming languages, and libraries) are being discovered faster than ever - sometimes even before the developers themselves identify them and release a fix," the pCloud Team said.

pCloud has also urged users to stay alert for phishing attacks that may trick recipients into entering their Master Password or recovery words by email, phone, or chat.

The development comes after numerous Reddit users reported receiving email alerts about a new device signing in to their pCloud Pass, despite having two-factor authentication (2FA) enabled, along with an option to "Open Settings" and change their Master Password if the action was not from them.

In all observed cases, a device named "pd" has been found to connect from the following two IP addresses belonging to pCloud's own infrastructure in Luxembourg -

45.131.244[.]7 ("eapi1.pcloud.com")

45.131.247[.]10 ("onlyoffice.pcloud.com")

However, it's unclear if the sign-in "attempts" were successful and if the attackers managed to access or download the encrypted vaults. pCloud also has not disclosed how the attackers got in, when the intrusion occurred, and for how long they had access before it was detected.

One Reddit user by the name "antenore" has also reported that the "Open settings" button in the email message "goes through pass.pcloud.com/track, but the base64 redirect inside it decodes to https://passqa1.pcloud.com/?#/preferences. That looks like an internal QA environment, and it does not load (ERR_CONNECTION_ABORTED)."

The Hacker News has contacted pCloud for comment, and we will update the story if we hear back.

This is the third security incident targeting password management software this month alone. At the start of November 2026, mSeven Software urged customers to change their passwords, reset 2FA, and remove their passkeys after detecting suspicious activity associated with its mSecure password management application.

Although the company did not provide any details as to what systems were compromised, whether customer data was accessed, and how many users were impacted, customers have since shared responses reportedly sent by mSeven that indicate an unknown threat actor "acquired an encrypted copy of a database containing information belonging to current and former users of the mSecure password management application."

The email, however, stated that there is "no evidence that the unauthorized actor has accessed the confidential information stored within the database in the mSecure Cloud. Please note, this issue only pertains to users that were synching with the mSecure Cloud."

Customers have also expressed dissatisfaction over the company's lack of transparency and explanations about how the breach took place. "Well it stinks that mSecure is completely silent here! Super unhelpful. I'm going on every website that has app reviews and giving mSecure the lowest rating. They're awful at handling this breach!," one user said.

"I feel like even if our data is still secure, I still do not like that [sic] lack of transparency and communication the company has provided," another user said. "Not a good way to run any business, especially one where trust is involved with passwords and documents."

On Wednesday, 1Password disclosed a security incident involving its backend systems after a threat actor compromised an employee's GitHub account and deployed malicious code to the company's backend services to steal some customer information.

Although the access did not extend to customer password vaults or other secrets, 1Password has not revealed how the employee's account was compromised. It also provided no details on when the intruder gained access, was detected, and was subsequently locked out.

[2] The Hacker News - Suspected Vietnam-Linked Hackers Target Southeast Asia in South China Sea Espionage Campaign
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Fri, 27 Nov 2026 22:06:11 +0530
Link: https://thehackernews.com/2026/11/suspected-vietnam-linked-hackers-target.html

[TAM METİN - 903 kelime]
A Vietnam-sponsored hacking group has been attributed to attacks targeting Southeast Asian military, government, and legislative organizations with an aim to gather intelligence on South China Sea defense and military agreements.

The previously undocumented incursion set, detailed by SOCRadar, has been codenamed WeaveLotus. The activity is assessed to have comprised five intrusions in Thailand and the Philippines and reconnaissance efforts against Indonesian government organizations.

"Initial access was achieved primarily by exploiting web application vulnerabilities and default credentials in open-source software, including GeoServer, JasperReports, osTicket, and exposed Laravel debug mode configurations," SOCRadar said in an analysis published Thursday.

"The threat actor established persistent internal network access using reverse SSH tunnels and moved laterally into downstream networks and applications."

The links to Vietnam are based on the presence of native Vietnamese-language code comments, operational activity aligning with UTC+07:00 working hours, regional strategic targeting, and custom scripting. The sustained campaign, codenamed RedOps, was observed between August and September 2026.

The threat actor has also been observed conducting open-source intelligence (OSINT), setting up automated Bing search scripts that scraped news and documents on military agreements involving the Philippines, China, and the U.S., with a particular emphasis on the South China Sea, a region of strategic and geopolitical importance.

Earlier this August, Hunt.io disclosed details of another cyber espionage operation undertaken by a suspected Chinese threat actor targeting a nuclear research organization and a marine engineering and shipbuilding company in the Philippines to steal sensitive documents after gaining access through known security vulnerabilities.

"Following the OSINT phase, the operators enumerated government, military, and legislative entities across the Philippines, Thailand, and Indonesia, compromising targets in Thailand and the Philippines across five confirmed intrusions," SOCRadar said. "Activity against Indonesian targets did not progress beyond reconnaissance."

"The core objective of RedOps appears to be the exfiltration of internal records and documents from Southeast Asian governments in support of Vietnam's foreign policy and military intelligence priorities."

A brief overview of the intrusion activity is as follows -

Thai military organization, which likely involved the exploitation of a critical GeoServer flaw (CVE-2024-36401, CVSS score: 9.8) to deploy a SOCKS5 proxy and open a reverse SSH tunnel, followed by hands-on reconnaissance, persistence, and unauthenticated access to Directus CMS API's file collection and asset download endpoints to enumerate records and download a subset of those records. The threat actor also ran Optical Character Recognition (OCR) on the collected data to extract English and Thai text from PNG images, and unsuccessfully attempted to gain access to surveillance video feeds hosted on Wowza to possibly gain real-time visibility into military operations.

Thai legislative organization, which involved the attacker leveraging an exposed JasperReports server with default administrative credentials (jasperadmin:jasperadmin) to upload a malicious report that ultimately executed SQL scripts on the backend Microsoft SQL Server and then deleted the report to cover up the tracks. The SQL commands, in turn, employed xp_cmdshell to launch a reverse SSH SOCKS proxy tunnel over port 443 to WeaveLotus infrastructure ("172.235.245[.]49"). The attackers deployed a lightweight Python C2 server to automate post-exploitation and targeted a third-party document management system (DMS) by launching password spraying attacks using real employee names paired with National ID numbers as passwords. Once authenticated, the attackers collected the entire system configuration, the complete application user directory comprising 1,502 records), along with system audit logs and 5,000 ZIP archives containing sensitive data.

Thai government organization, which involved the attacker gaining root SSH access to a server running Keycloak 3.4 and dumping its credential database, following cracking the PBKDF2-SHA256 hashes offline with wordlists built around Thai Buddhist Era years and then validating them in real-time against the Resource Owner Password Credentials (ROPC) token endpoint. The working accounts belonged to users from various Thai ministries and agencies, with two of them granting the attackers administrative privileges. The valid credentials were then used to pull 349,782 records from a downstream application that contained national strategy alignment, reform directives, masterplan targets, sustainable development goals, and five-year fiscal plans, among others.

Philippine government organization A, which involved the exploitation of an unauthenticated SQL injection flaw in osTicket (CVE-2021-42235, CVSS score: 9.8) to extract database schemas and dump usernames, email addresses, and bcrypt password hashes for registered staff accounts.

Philippine government organization B, which probed a web application built on the Laravel framework to obtain server paths and fragments of application source code, ultimately revealing directories tied to an internal project-management and budgeting system.

"WeaveLotus conducted broad reconnaissance against Indonesian government agencies [between August and September 2026]," SOCRadar said.

"The operators began with passive reconnaissance, harvesting Certificate Transparency (CT) logs via CertSpotter and crt.sh, before progressing to active Nmap port scans, VPN service fingerprinting (targeting Palo Alto GlobalProtect, Ivanti Connect Secure / Pulse Secure, Cisco ASA, and Fortinet FortiGate), and IMAP/POP3 banner grabbing across more than 30 organizations."

The RedOps campaign is notable for the fact that it has only singled out government, military, and legislative institutions in the Philippines, Thailand, and Indonesia, with no evidence that private-sector entities were targeted. This indicates that the activity is consistent with state-directed intelligence collection.

"The RedOps campaign illustrates how a suspected Vietnam-linked espionage operation leveraged exposed applications, weak authentication controls, and persistent network tunnels to conduct intelligence collection across Southeast Asian government and military environments," SOCRadar said.

"The recovered tooling and operational artifacts provide valuable insight into the actor's methods, while the available evidence supports tracking WeaveLotus as a distinct activity cluster without conclusively establishing state sponsorship."

[3] The Hacker News - Microsoft Store App Found Delivering Vinci RAT Capable of Remote System Control
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Fri, 27 Nov 2026 17:19:34 +0530
Link: https://thehackernews.com/2026/11/microsoft-store-app-found-delivering.html

[TAM METİN - 476 kelime]
Cybersecurity researchers have disclosed details of a cyber attack in which a threat actor leveraged a Microsoft Store application to deliver a remote access trojan (RAT) called Vinci RAT.

The intrusion, according to eSentire, begins with a redirection to a bogus site for a sniping tool ("snipp.codes-team[.]com"), from where a Microsoft-signed installer called "Snipping-Tool.exe" is downloaded. Executing the binary leads to the installation of Setup Assistant, an Electron-based application published on the Microsoft Store by ETPS Ltd.

"The application was heavily obfuscated, maintained communication with C2 [command-and-control] infrastructure, monitored the victim's Downloads folder, and supported execution of C2-supplied PowerShell and JavaScript," eSentire said.

"This access was used to launch a malicious PowerShell stager, establish persistence through a scheduled task, and retrieve a previously undocumented PowerShell-based RAT."

Once installed, Setup Assistant acts as a backdoor and is designed to run obfuscated JavaScript that allows it to contact the C2 server at "setup-assistant[.]com" and retrieve arbitrary PowerShell or JavaScript code, ultimately resulting in the deployment of the RAT by means of a stager.

Vinci RAT, as the malware is called, is equipped to fingerprint the host, enumerate installed security tools, facilitate remote and reverse shell access, deliver files, and execute shellcode, DLL, EXE, and PowerShell payloads.

Interestingly, the arbitrary PowerShell or JavaScript execution is triggered by means of two methods -

Monitor for file names in the victim's Downloads directory by matching them against a predefined list specified in a C2-retrieved configuration ("MSTeamsSetup.exe" and "Reader_install.exe"). If a match is found, it sends an HTTP POST request to the C2 server and executes the response.

The C2 server initiates an update action via the URL setup-assistant[.]com/api/update-status?device=<victim_id>"

The Vinci RAT stager is responsible for running a PowerShell command that disables the Windows Antimalware Scan Interface (AMSI), generates a unique victim identifier, establishes communication with a C2 server ("monkeysholder[.]info/api.php"), set up persistence via a scheduled task that runs every 10 minutes, and downloads the RAT payload.

Further analysis of the root domain "codes-team[.]com" has identified additional sub-domains that suggest that the threat actors are spoofing software download pages for legitimate applications like Microsoft Teams and Roblox -

roblx.codes-team[.]com

teams.codes-team[.]com

teamsmac.codes-team[.]com

teams-bing.codes-team[.]com

As of writing, Setup Assistant is no longer accessible on Microsoft Software. It was first released on June 1, 2026, while the "codes-team[.]com" domain was registered in late March 2026.

It remains unclear whether the threat actors themselves took down the app in response to the public disclosure or whether Microsoft removed it, as eSentire noted that the tech giant had failed to take action despite reporting the app through its abuse form on November 10, 2026, responding that it was "not a violation of our terms."

"The campaign highlights how attackers are combining search-engine redirection, fake software download pages, obfuscated Electron applications, and trusted software distribution channels to gain persistent access to victim environments," the Canadian cybersecurity company said.

[4] The Hacker News - How to Keep Your Security Picture Current When Attackers Move at AI Speed
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Fri, 27 Nov 2026 16:02:18 +0530
Link: https://thehackernews.com/2026/11/how-to-keep-your-security-picture.html

[TAM METİN - 972 kelime]
Most security metrics are not built to be used. They are built to be shown. Somewhere between the quarterly review and the next status update, a set of numbers gets assembled that looks reassuring in a room and tells almost no one what is actually happening across the environment that week. Someone pulls them together the week before, cleans them up, and presents a picture of a program that is, as of that one morning, fine.

This is not a failure of effort. Security teams are doing the work, often times more of it than anyone outside the team realizes. The problem is that the reporting cycle rewards the snapshot, and a snapshot is a memory, not a measurement. It tells you how things looked on the day you looked. It says very little about the weeks in between, and risk does not keep to a reporting schedule.

Gathering the Picture Is the Hard Part, and It Is Getting Harder

The conversation about security metrics usually jumps straight to which numbers matter. That skips the part that actually hurts. Before anyone can report a single metric, someone has to go and gather it, and today that is a genuinely painful job.

The information needed to describe a security posture is scattered across dozens of systems that were never meant to talk to each other. Cloud accounts, identity providers, endpoints, code repositories, ticketing tools, each one holds a piece. Pulling those pieces together usually means logging into one console after another, exporting spreadsheets, taking screenshots, and stitching it all by hand into something presentable. It takes days. It eats the time of people who were hired to do security, not data entry. And by the time the picture is assembled, parts of it are already out of date, because the environment kept changing while the deck was being built.

So the metric that lands in the review is not really a measurement of where things stand. It is a measurement of where things stood, filtered through however long it took to collect. (And filtered, quietly, toward whatever looked defensible that week.)

Attackers Are Not Working on a Quarterly Cadence. With AI, They Are Faster Than Ever.

Here is why the stale snapshot has gone from a weakness to a real liability. The people trying to get in have changed speed.

AI has handed attackers a serious acceleration. Reconnaissance that used to take a skilled operator days now runs in minutes. Phishing that used to read as obviously fake now arrives fluent and personalized at scale. Known weaknesses get found and probed almost as fast as they appear. The attack side has automated the slow parts, and the result is adversaries who move in hours, iterate constantly, and do not pause politely between your reporting cycles.

Now put the two timelines next to each other. On one side, a picture of your security that is gathered by hand, a few times a year, and is slightly stale the moment it is finished. On the other, an adversary moving at machine speed, every day, including the long quiet stretch between your last review and your next one. That gap was always uncomfortable. Against AI-accelerated attackers, it is the whole game.

The Real Shift Is From Reporting to Visibility

The fix is not a better slide. It is being able to see where you actually stand, continuously, without a person having to go assemble it first.

That is worth saying plainly, because it is easy to confuse with detection. This is not about catching the attacker in the act. It is about never losing sight of your own environment in the first place, knowing what you have, what is covered, what changed, and where the gaps are, as a living picture rather than a quarterly photograph. Continuous visibility into the real environment, pulled straight from the systems that already hold the data, is what turns a security posture from something you reconstruct under deadline into something you can simply look at, any day, and trust.

This is the problem Anecdotes was built to solve. Instead of a person logging into one console after another and rebuilding the picture by hand, it connects directly to the systems a company already runs, the cloud accounts, identity providers, code repositories, and the hundreds of other tools where the real state actually lives, and pulls the information straight from the source. The picture assembles itself and stays current, so the question stops being how many days it will take to gather and becomes simply what the data says right now. And to be clear about what that is and is not, it does not chase the attacker for you. It makes sure you are never the last to know what your own environment looks like.

When the picture is always current, the work changes shape. Nobody spends the week before the review gathering. The number is just there, today's number, and the team spends its time acting on what it says instead of proving it exists.

None of this requires rebuilding everything at once, and anyone who says it does is selling something. Pick one thing worth seeing clearly, coverage, say, or how long exposures stay open. Connect it to the source so it keeps itself current. Watch it for a month, and let it tell you something uncomfortable.

Security is not a thing that is true on review day. It is true, or it is not, on an ordinary Tuesday, when no one is looking and last quarter's slide is gathering dust in a shared drive. And on that Tuesday, the attackers are not waiting. The teams that stay ahead are not the ones with the best-looking dashboards. They are the ones who can see where they stand at any moment, without having to go dig for it first.

(XXXXXXX, AÇIK - https://thehackernews.com/2026/11/how-to-keep-your-security-picture.html, thehackernews.com, 27.11.2026)

[5] The Hacker News - Hackers Trojanize Official Q-Dir and DesktopOK Downloads to Deliver Remote Access Malware
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Fri, 27 Nov 2026 14:59:30 +0530
Link: https://thehackernews.com/2026/11/hackers-trojanize-official-q-dir-and.html

[TAM METİN - 574 kelime]
Cybersecurity researchers have disclosed details of a software supply chain attack that has tampered with Q-Dir and DesktopOK installation files distributed via the official SoftwareOK website.

"Evidence indicates that malicious files were generated not only when users downloaded the programs directly from the official download page but also during the update process of previously installed legitimate programs," South Korean cybersecurity company AhnLab said.

Q-Dir, also called the Quad Directory Explorer, is a free, lightweight file manager for Windows developed by SoftwareOK. According to its website, DesktopOK is a free Windows utility designed to save and restore desktop icon positions.

The unauthorized modifications are designed such that the malicious activity is challenging to detect when running the programs, while simultaneously retrieving and deploying a Python-based remote control malware (RAT) from an external server.

Once installed, the RAT payload is capable of executing shell commands and Python code, transferring files, capturing screenshots, and collecting system metadata based on commands issued by the attacker.

The following product versions have been confirmed to be poisoned, given the discrepancies in the SHA-256 hashes of the installer files and the presence of additional code that triggers additional file downloads and automatic execution -

The exact mechanism that allowed the installation files to be replaced with a trojanized version remains unknown.

"The malicious installer runs the legitimate Q-Dir or DesktopOK to lower the user's suspicion, while simultaneously downloading and installing a Python RAT package from an external server," AhnLab said.

Specifically, this involves downloading two payloads from "mail.c1a2[.]top" -

7zr.exe, the legitimate 7-Zip tool to decompress files

dsv2_py39_agent.7z, a package containing the RAT and Python runtime environment

The attack chain is fairly straightforward in that it uses the 7-Zip tool binary to extract and run the Python RAT. The malware then sets up persistence on the host by means of a scheduled task that impersonates Microsoft Edge to fly under the radar. The task is configured to execute the RAT by launching a Python script named "py_client_dsv2.py."

Upon execution, the RAT establishes communication with its command-and-control (C2) server ("8.210.69[.]51:9876") to receive tasks and run them. It supports the following features -

Shell command execution

Python code execution

File upload and download

Operating system and system information

It also gathers a list of running processes and compares it against an internal list of various security products used in South Korea and abroad, including AhnLab, ESTSecurity, Hauri, CrowdStrike, SentinelOne, Trend Micro, Avast, McAfee, ESET, Symantec, Kaspersky, Bitdefender, Sophos, Fortinet, Elastic, and Malwarebytes.

Information about identified security programs is transmitted to the C2 server, so as to help the attackers better plan the next stages of the attack or serve payloads that can bypass detection.

The activity has not been attributed to any known threat actor. However, AhnLab said it identified Chinese language comments in the source code, suggesting that the malware authors or attackers may have used a Chinese-speaking environment.

"Users who are currently using Q-Dir or DesktopOK, or who have recently installed or updated these programs, should verify the hash values of the downloaded files and check their systems for any traces of RAT installation or execution," AhnLab said.

"However, simply uninstalling and reinstalling the program may not remove a Python RAT that has already been installed. Therefore, you should also check for suspicious Python execution environments, scheduled tasks, related files, and traces of external network communication, update your antivirus engine to the latest version, and then perform a full system scan."

[6] BleepingComputer - New Boston Scientific data breach exposes personal, medical info
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Fri, 27 Nov 2026 13:57:42 -0500
Link: https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/new-boston-scientific-data-breach-exposes-personal-medical-info/

[TAM METİN - 497 kelime]
Medical technology company Boston Scientific is sending data breach notices to individuals affected by a cyberattack on the firm.

According to a notification sample Boston Scientific shared with U.S. authorities, the hacker used social engineering in a voice phishing (vishing) attack to gain access to the company's cloud infrastructure.

The attack exposed cloud-based applications and cloud-hosted data between May 7 and 1.

The company became aware of the unauthorized access on May 12, and the investigation determined that a data breach occurred on November 5.

Individuals in multiple U.S. states have been impacted, exposing personal details and medical information. According to the Texas Attorney General breach portal, the incident exposed information from 117,789 people, which included:

Social Security Number (SSN)

Government-issued ID number (e.g., passport, state ID card)

Health insurance information

The letter sent to impacted individuals specifies that patient medical records and data from remote monitoring systems, connected medical devices, and clinical case management or workflow applications were not exposed.

Boston Scientific is a Massachusetts-based medical device maker whose products include stents, catheters, pacemakers, defibrillators, and endoscopes.

It is one of the world’s largest medical device manufacturers, with more than 59,000 employees, 13 manufacturing facilities, and a presence in 127 countries. In 2025, Boston Scientific reported more than $20 billion in revenue.

The company said there was no disruption to manufacturing, ordering, distribution, or service to patients and healthcare providers as a result of this incident.

It also said the incident had no impact on operations, performance, remote monitoring, or the safety of its products and medical devices.

Boston Scientific says it contained the incident, investigated with outside cybersecurity experts, and notified law enforcement. The notification does not offer free credit monitoring to impacted individuals but recommends using free credit reports and considering placing fraud alerts and credit freezes.

Second breach in 2026

The breach is the second cybersecurity incident this year. On August 25, Boston Scientific disclosed a cyberattack that caused network outages and a global operational disruption for the company, including to order processing and shipping.

CrowdStrike’s investigation determined that the attacker breached an internet-facing network device and pivoted to a limited part of the on-premises IT environment. However, the investigators found no evidence that the attackers compromised cloud applications.

BleepingComputer contacted Boston Scientific to learn more about the most recent incident, and a spokesperson confirmed that it is unrelated to the August 2026 intrusion.

The spokesperson declined to share additional details.

[7] BleepingComputer - Unpatched Cacti zero-day with public PoC gives remote code execution
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Fri, 27 Nov 2026 12:06:13 -0500
Link: https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/unpatched-cacti-zero-day-with-public-poc-gives-remote-code-execution/

[TAM METİN - 655 kelime]
A zero-day vulnerability in the Cacti network monitoring platform has been publicly disclosed, along with a proof-of-concept exploit that lets unauthenticated attackers execute commands on vulnerable servers.

The exploit, dubbed "4HTTP2SHELL" (previously 4HTTP2ROOT), was published on Thanksgiving Day by a cybersecurity researcher known as ek0ms savi0r from the Church of Malware, who demonstrated how the vulnerability can be exploited using just four HTTP requests.

Cacti is an open-source network monitoring and graphing tool used to monitor the performance of servers, routers, switches, firewalls, and other network infrastructure.

Because the software can store credentials and SNMP passphrases used to access or read data from monitored devices, compromising a Cacti server could potentially give attackers access to other systems on a corporate network.

The researcher published a technical write-up, a proof-of-concept (PoC) exploit, and a video demonstrating the attack in a GitHub repository named "Sad Girls 0day Club."

"I found a path from a fresh, unauthenticated HTTP session to command execution as the Cacti server user in four requests. No login. No valid API key. No phishing. Just four HTTP requests against the default guest surface," explains the researcher.

The vulnerability stems from weak input validation that allows an unauthenticated attacker to inject SQL statements into the application's database and modify commands executed by Cacti's real-time poller.

The attack requires the Cacti guest account to be enabled but does not require attackers to possess valid credentials.

While the researcher demonstrated the exploit providing root access to a vulnerable server, cybersecurity firm watchTowr explains that the privileges gained through exploitation depend on the account running the Cacti web server and poller processes.

In many deployments, this could be a low-privileged web server account rather than root.

Cybersecurity firm RavenAI explains that the attack abuses weak validation of guest users' graph display settings, resulting in a stored SQL injection vulnerability.

The attack involves an attacker saving malicious SQL statements in a guest user's graph display preferences, which are stored in Cacti's database.

When Cacti processes these saved preferences, it executes the SQL statements, allowing the attacker to modify the poller's configuration and cause it to run malicious operating system commands.

The researcher tested the exploit against Cacti Core 1.2.31, but it is unclear whether earlier versions are also vulnerable.

In a separate post on the GitHub repository, ek0ms savi0r expressed frustration with how companies handle vulnerability disclosures, describing years of security reports being dismissed or closed as duplicates, expected behavior, or issues that could not be reproduced.

"Security researchers are not the bad guys. We're the ones trying to help. We're the ones who read the docs, wrote the repro, and sent the packet capture," wrote the researcher.

"We get cracked because we get gaslit for years by billion dollar companies that treat disclosure like a PR problem."

In an Instagram post announcing the Cacti exploit, the researcher shared a video demonstration of the PoC and said that the vulnerability was not reported to Cacti before being released.

Until a patch is released, administrators are advised to deactivate the Cacti guest account and restrict access to the application's web interface to trusted networks.

Administrators should also review poller configurations for unexpected commands and examine application logs for warnings involving non-numeric values in graph display settings, which could indicate attempted exploitation.

As of November 27, there have been no reports of the vulnerability being exploited in attacks.

[8] BleepingComputer - US Air Force cloud platform reportedly hacked in latest federal breach
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Fri, 27 Nov 2026 10:09:40 -0500
Link: https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/us-air-force-cloud-platform-reportedly-hacked-in-latest-federal-breach/

[TAM METİN - 734 kelime]
Hackers have reportedly breached the US Air Force's Cloud One computing platform, adding to a recent wave of cyberattacks affecting US government agencies, including the FBI, CIA, and Treasury Department.

According to Reuters, the intrusion was discovered within the past month during an upgrade of the cloud platform, disrupting application migrations and software updates.

Launched in 2017, Cloud One is an enterprise cloud computing platform managed by the US Air Force that allows Department of Defense agencies to host and manage applications supporting military operations, personnel management, logistics, and maintenance.

According to one source who spoke to Reuters, the hackers exploited an unidentified software vulnerability to gain access to the platform, potentially exposing sensitive military operational and logistics information.

US Cyber Command has reportedly been called in to assess the impact, with the attackers, scope of the attack, and whether any data was stolen currently unknown.

The Air Force declined to comment on the security or operational status of the affected systems, telling Reuters that it continuously takes measures to protect its information, systems, and mission capabilities.

The reported Air Force breach follows other cybersecurity incidents disclosed over the past week targeting US federal agencies.

On November 21, it was revealed that hackers reportedly breached the FBI's National Crime Information Center (NCIC), a nationwide database used by local, state, and federal law enforcement agencies.

The database contains information on wanted and missing persons, outstanding arrest warrants, stolen vehicles and firearms, and other criminal justice records.

The intrusion came two months after the ShinyHunters extortion group breached FBI systems by exploiting an unpatched Oracle PeopleSoft vulnerability in the agency's recruitment website, FBIJobs.gov.

Earlier this week, hackers also reportedly breached an open-source intelligence (OSINT) analysis system at the Central Intelligence Agency (CIA). The incident also affected the Office of the Director of National Intelligence (ODNI) and the National Geospatial-Intelligence Agency (NGA).

Hours after the CIA breach was reported, the US Treasury Department's Financial Crimes Enforcement Network (FinCEN) disclosed that it was investigating unauthorized access to its systems.

It is unclear whether any of the recently disclosed federal government breaches are connected.

AI-powered hacking tools fuel cyberattacks

The CIA breach was reportedly conducted using the ARTEX AI-powered penetration testing platform, which the FBI recently warned was being used to automate cyberattacks worldwide.

In an advisory, the FBI said multiple threat actors had used ARTEX since at least August 2026 in attacks targeting government, defense, healthcare, financial, and technology organizations.

ARTEX is an open-source penetration testing framework that uses AI agents to automate various stages of a cyberattack, including discovering and exploiting vulnerabilities, escalating privileges, and stealing data.

The framework integrates with external AI models from Anthropic Claude, OpenAI GPT, DeepSeek, Grok, and others, allowing attackers to conduct autonomous attacks against organizations with minimal human intervention.

According to the FBI, some ARTEX-powered intrusions have progressed from initial targeting to privileged access in less than three hours.

The platform was used by a Chinese state-aligned threat actor tracked by Microsoft as Storm-0940 in an October attack targeting ConnectWise's Cloud Backup service. Cybersecurity firm Huntress identified at least 162 compromised Microsoft 365 customer tenants during the campaign.

Other attacks linked to ARTEX include:

Attacks against South Korean financial institutions that exposed personal information of approximately 67,000 people.

A South Korean hacking campaign targeting more than 90 organizations, resulting in the theft of approximately 8.2 million data records and the arrest of seven suspects.

A cyberespionage campaign attributed by CrowdStrike to a Chinese hacking group tracked as Vapor Panda, which targeted more than 200 organizations across 14 countries and compromised at least nine.

As of early November, researchers had identified more than 1,600 publicly exposed ARTEX management consoles, with the number believed to be much higher.

It remains unknown whether ARTEX or other AI-powered hacking tools were used in the attacks against the Air Force, FBI, or FinCEN.

[9] BleepingComputer - Your Firewall Isn't Enough: The Hidden Risk of Unmanaged VMs
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Fri, 27 Nov 2026 10:00:10 -0500
Link: https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/your-firewall-isnt-enough-the-hidden-risk-of-unmanaged-vms/

[TAM METİN - 1304 kelime]
When discussing IT infrastructure security, the focus is usually on firewalls, EDR, backup, vulnerability management, and network segmentation. However, all of them are effective only under one condition — if the infrastructure itself is under control.

Incidents often begin not with the exploitation of a vulnerability or bypassing protection systems, but with shortcomings in basic infrastructure management. Every virtual machine, server, access, and IP address must have an owner, a lifetime, and a controlled path for creation and deletion.

When management processes are opaque, the infrastructure itself becomes a source of information security risks, and security tools have to operate over an unknown environment and merely contain the consequences.

This principle is equally critical for enterprise companies and service providers — regardless of whether the platform serves internal services, development teams, or external customers, a loss of control over administration can lead to the compromise of the entire environment.

Why Security Grows Out of Manageability

In modern IT infrastructure, hundreds of systems run on the same resources, and the environment itself is constantly changing — dozens of services are created, scaled, and deleted daily.

In such conditions, provisioning comes to the fore. Deploying a new virtual machine without a regulation and recording the owner leaves "forgotten" VMs in the environment. They do not receive updates, fall out of monitoring, and become an easy entry point for an attacker.

Centralized access management is no less critical. The platform must delineate the rights of users and administrators and record all operations; otherwise, excessive rights accumulate, allowing attackers to move laterally across the network.

We should not forget about the isolation of environments — the resources of different customers at a hosting provider, or internal systems, external services, and development environments in corporate IT infrastructure. If isolation is violated, an incident in the test environment can affect business-critical services.

All this is impossible without lifecycle control — linking capacity allocation to billing, quotas, and timely deletion of resources, so that outdated accounts and "abandoned" IP addresses do not remain in the network.

Finally, manageability is impossible without accurate accounting of the physical layer: the appearance, decommissioning, and movement of servers between racks and sites. Inaccurate accounting generates unaccounted servers and loss of control over security configurations.

Therefore, the foundation of information security is the transparency of resource management throughout their entire lifecycle.

Bring Virtualization and Infrastructure Management Under Control

ISPsystem brings VM management, physical infrastructure inventory, and resource lifecycle workflows together in one environment.

See how VMmanager, DCImanager, and BILLmanager help teams gain visibility and control across their IT infrastructure.

Migration from VMware and the Change in the Management Model

Broadcom's change in licensing policy forced companies to reconsider their infrastructure strategies and look for alternatives to VMware — for example, considering Proxmox VE, OpenStack, and other open-source solutions. However, migrating to a new platform requires more than just the correct transfer of virtual machines.

Over the years of working with VMware, many have become accustomed to perceiving it as a single whole — virtualization, resource management, access, automation, and accounting.

When migrating to another stack, it quickly becomes clear that virtualization is not equal to infrastructure management: the hypervisor is just one element, and the remaining functions have to be assembled from several independent solutions.

Proxmox and OpenStack solve virtualization tasks on their own, but without additional software, they do not cover operational processes and control over the other layers of the environment. As a result, organizations have to independently integrate equipment management systems, resource accounting, access control, and inventory.

The more individual components in the infrastructure, the harder it is to maintain unified security policies — risks arise of losing a unified RBAC, end-to-end inventory, action auditing, and automation.

Therefore, it is important to view migration from VMware not just as a change of hypervisor, but as a revision of the entire infrastructure management model: if centralized control, automation, and lifecycle management are postponed for later, over time they become a source of new risks.

Which Processes a Management Platform Must Cover

After abandoning VMware, companies face the main question — how to rebuild infrastructure management.

When choosing a replacement, it is important to look not only at the capabilities of the hypervisor itself but also at what operational and security processes the chosen solution can cover and what additional integrations will be required.

At the foundation, there should be centralized management of the entire environment from a single interface and role-based access control (RBAC).

Equally important are the isolation of environments or customers, transparent resource accounting, and control of the entire lifecycle — from the creation of a virtual machine to its deletion and the revocation of access.

Also, the platform must manage the physical layer of servers and automate routine operations.

Such an architecture can be built in different ways: assembled from separate open-source tools and independently solving the issue of their integration, or by choosing a ready-made comprehensive solution.

Regardless of the approach, it is precisely the coverage of these processes that returns the infrastructure to the transparency and manageability that the VMware ecosystem previously provided.

How to Build a Unified Management Environment

To avoid assembling infrastructure management from disparate systems, all these processes can be combined within a single ISPsystem product stack.

In this architecture, VMmanager is responsible for virtualization, DCImanager for the physical level of the infrastructure, and BILLmanager acts as the end-to-end layer for resource provisioning, quotas, accounting, and FinOps.

VMmanager automates the lifecycle of virtual machines, including deployment with a preinstalled software stack, and scales up to 60,000 VMs and 700 nodes in a single environment.

Network isolation based on VxLAN supports more than 16 million isolated segments between geographically distributed sites, and a self-service portal is provided for users.

In parallel, DCImanager provides centralized management and full inventory of the physical layer in multi-vendor environments — from servers to switches and UPSs. The platform checks equipment health and disk S.M.A.R.T. data, configures BMC, performs secure drive wiping, automatically installs the OS, monitors hardware status, and controls warranty periods.

All three products integrate seamlessly with each other and work as a single environment, and if necessary, can interact with external solutions — for example, with OpenStack. BILLmanager acts as the end-to-end layer here: it links the provisioning of IT resources with authorization and accounting, allows assembling combined services, and implements showback and chargeback mechanisms.

The built-in BI module with customizable dashboards for analyzing resource utilization deserves special attention. In a corporate environment, it helps to find "abandoned" capacity in time, which development teams forget to delete after completing tasks — such servers do not sit idle without updates and control, and the company reduces hardware and license costs.

How Implementing a Unified Management Environment Affects IT Infrastructure Security

When the processes of managing virtualization, physical equipment, and the resource lifecycle are linked into a single environment, security ceases to be just a set of protective tools and becomes the result of systemic manageability.

Information security and operations teams get a transparent picture of the entire environment — from the physical server to the virtual machine, its owner, and the history of changes, which directly reduces the number of "blind spots."

Automation of provisioning, delineation of rights, and control over resource lifetimes reduce the risk of errors in issuing access, the appearance of unaccounted servers, and the retention of "forgotten" virtual machines. This also simplifies the migration from VMware itself: companies do not just replace the hypervisor, but build a transparent and manageable operating model.

As a result, infrastructure security relies not only on individual security tools but also on the management system itself, which makes the environment predictable, controllable, and resilient to risks.

Ready to build a transparent and secure infrastructure? Discover how ISPsystem can help you regain full control over your resources.

Sponsored and written by ISPsystem.

[11] BleepingComputer - Microsoft removes WSUS server role in Windows Server 2028
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Fri, 27 Nov 2026 06:18:36 -0500
Link: https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/microsoft/microsoft-removes-wsus-server-role-in-windows-server-2028/

[TAM METİN - 516 kelime]
Microsoft will remove the WSUS server role from Windows Server 2028, the next major release of its server operating system.

Introduced in 2005 as Software Update Services (SUS), WSUS (Windows Server Update Services) lets IT administrators manage and distribute updates for Microsoft products across corporate networks with many Windows devices.

It gives admins centralized control over the updates, rather than having each device download them individually from Microsoft's servers.

Microsoft has officially deprecated WSUS in September 2024, but said that it still plans to maintain functionality and continue publishing updates through the channel. The change affects enterprise environments that rely on WSUS to manage updates delivered to many devices but does not impact home users or those using Microsoft Configuration Manager.

In a recently updated support document, Microsoft went even further and confirmed that Windows Server 2028 removes the WSUS server role altogether.

"WSUS remains available and supported in earlier Windows Server releases, including Windows Server 2025, for the duration of each release's support lifecycle. You can continue to use WSUS on those releases to manage updates for supported Windows client and Windows Server releases, which includes updates to Windows Server 2028," it said.

"Microsoft recognizes the need to manage updates in disconnected and highly controlled environments. We're invested in supporting these scenarios and we'll share more as the work progresses."

Businesses are now encouraged to adopt cloud-based solutions, such as Windows Autopatch, Microsoft Intune, and Azure Update Manager, for keeping endpoints and servers on their network up to date.

"While the WSUS role remains available in Windows Server 2025, we recommend organizations transition to cloud tools, including including Windows Autopatch and Microsoft Intune for client update management and Azure Update Manager for server update management," Microsoft's Nir Froimovici said in September 2024. "Remember: WSUS remains operational but is no longer investing in new features."

Windows Server 2028 also removes VBScript (disabled by default in Internet Explorer 11 on Windows 10) and the legacy Windows Management Instrumentation Command-line (WMIC) utility (deprecated in Windows Server 2012 and Windows 10 21H1) to thwart a wide range of malware and attack tactics abusing them.

Windows Internet Name Service (WINS) support, deprecated with the release of Windows Server 2022 in August 2021, is also being removed in the next Windows Server version, as Microsoft announced a year ago.

Microsoft announced Windows Server 2028 last week at the Ignite 2026 conference, and it can be tested by registering for the Windows Insider Program for Windows Server or applying to enroll in the Windows Server Technology Adoption Program (TAP) for earlier access.

[12] BleepingComputer - SAP confirms security incident after suspicious GitHub activity
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Fri, 27 Nov 2026 04:56:36 -0500
Link: https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/sap-confirms-security-incident-after-suspicious-github-activity/

[TAM METİN - 464 kelime]
SAP has confirmed to BleepingComputer that it took urgent action to protect its customers after detecting suspicious activity in its IT environment.

The German software giant said its evidence so far does not show downstream compromise of customer systems or data.

Der Spiegel reported earlier today, based on an internal SAP memo it saw, that the software firm was hit by a cyberattack it tracks under the code name “Project Granite.”

The software company reportedly asked all employees to keep the project details confidential.

According to the German outlet, sources within the company said the attackers breached SAP’s GitHub environment, possibly with help from AI tools.

Insider sources say that the attacker managed to steal authentication tokens from SAP employees and leveraged them to copy and also modify SAP source code on GitHub.

BleepingComputer has contacted SAP for a statement on the reported breach, and the company acknowledged the incident and said it had taken steps to protect its customers.

“SAP has identified suspicious activity and promptly implemented the necessary measures to protect its customers and preserve the security of its services,” a company representative told BleepingComputer.

“Based on our investigation to date, we have found no evidence of unauthorized access to customer business data.”

“Our customers trust us to protect their data, and that remains our highest priority. In a highly dynamic threat environment, we will continue to strengthen our security to provide the secure and resilient environment our customers depend on.”

SAP did not specify the environments where the suspicious activity was observed, or whether customers downloading code from its GitHub repositories or performing updates that are directly fetched or indirectly fed by those repositories should take any action.

BleepingComputer has asked SAP about this, but a spokesperson declined to share any further information.

Meanwhile, Germany’s Federal Office for Information Security (BSI) issued a public warning yesterday about agentic AI tools that empower cyberattacks, such as ARTEX, CAIRN, and PentAGI.

BSI cautioned organizations in the country to focus on responding to security incidents and ensure they follow all recommended security-strengthening practices.

This warning comes shortly after the national cybersecurity agencies in Switzerland and Italy reported the first detections of large-scale AI-driven cyberattacks targeting organizations in the critical infrastructure sector.

[13] BleepingComputer - Microsoft pushes emergency hotpatch to fix Windows Server crashes
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Fri, 27 Nov 2026 04:08:16 -0500
Link: https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/microsoft/new-out-of-band-oob-emergency-hotpatch-fixes-windows-server-2025-crashes/

[TAM METİN - 387 kelime]
Microsoft has released an emergency hotpatch update to address Windows Server 2025 crashes triggered after installing this month's security hotpatch update (KB5131077).

According to Microsoft, the KB5138318 out-of-band update should address stop errors with 0x139 codes triggered on affected servers when IPv6 routes expire.

"Microsoft has released a new Windows out-of-band (OOB) update today, November 25, 2026, to address an issue affecting a limited number of Windows Servers with Router Advertisement (RA) enabled that receive time-bound IPv6 routes," it said in a message center update.

"Affected devices might stop unexpectedly with stop error 0x139 (KERNEL_SECURITY_CHECK_FAILURE) when a route timer expires. This issue affects some Windows Server 2025 devices after installing the November 2026 Windows security hotpatch update."

This emergency non-security update is now rolling out to affected Windows Server 2025 devices enrolled in hotpatch updates through Windows Update.

KB5138318 will download and install automatically and will take effect without requiring IT administrators or users to restart affected devices.

The November 2026 hotpatch updates are also causing issues for Windows 11 users, who are experiencing installation and launch issues affecting Office and third-party applications, as well as printing issues on systems running Forcepoint's DLP Endpoint Agent data loss prevention solution after installing this month's hotpatch on Windows 11 systems.

Windows Autopatch reached public preview in early June after an initial unveil in April 2022 and has been generally available since July 2022.

Microsoft announced earlier this year, in March, that it would turn on hotpatch security updates by default for all eligible Windows devices managed through the Microsoft Graph API and Microsoft Intune, after the May 2026 Windows security updates are installed.

One month later, Microsoft fixed a Windows Autopatch bug that was causing driver updates restricted by administrative policies to deploy on some Autopatch-managed Windows devices in the European Union.

[14] Security Affairs - Fake Government App Exposes Italy’s Expanding Spyware Surveillance Industry
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Sat, 28 Nov 2026 00:08:30 +0000
Link: https://securityaffairs.com/204142/intelligence/fake-government-app-exposes-italys-expanding-spyware-surveillance-industry.html

[TAM METİN - 744 kelime]
Fake Government App Exposes Italy’s Expanding Spyware Surveillance Industry

A fake Interior Ministry app used Morpheus spyware to target asylum seekers in Italy, raising questions about surveillance and oversight.

An Android app called FirmaDigitale promised asylum seekers news on their protection status, instead, it was delivering spyware.

An IrpiMedia investigation by Rosita Rijtano builds on Lookout’s 2024 discovery and attributes the malware to IPS, a Latina supplier to prosecutors, police and intelligence agencies. Nobody knows who ordered the operation or whom it hit.

Victims downloaded the app from firmadigitale.assistenza-mobile[.]it, a page mimicking the website of Italy’s primary postal service provider Poste Italiane. The app displayed the Interior Ministry logo and mentioned a protection decree. Police deny any link to it, and Poste didn’t comment.

The spyware could add components to use the microphone, intercept internet calls, record the screen and track the phone. It could also reach contacts, call logs, files and texts. The site has been unreachable since 2025, but the app could still circulate elsewhere.

Osservatorio Nessuno named the spyware Morpheus in April 2026 after finding it in a fake Fastweb app. Attackers also abused the TIM, Iliad, PosteMobile and WindTre brands, and Iliad said it knew of no such apps. Morpheus can even hijack WhatsApp through a fake biometric prompt.

“We are faced with yet another example of low-cost spyware that exploits vulnerability and suffering to strike at one of every individual’s most intimate rights: the privacy of the devices we use daily for all our activities and communications,” comments a spokesperson for Osservatorio Nessuno, a volunteer organization that studies digital surveillance technologies and provides technical assistance to journalists and activists.

“This case is particularly cruel because it leverages—from a position of supreme power—systems that are already discriminatory and hostile, specifically targeting migrants. The responsibility lies in many quarters: from the complete lack of Italian or European regulation regarding the proliferation of such companies, to a total absence of transparency and constraints—including ethical ones—on these types of operations.”

Kristina Balaam of Citizen Lab, formerly at Lookout, says social engineering makes these tools cheaper than zero-click spyware and still effective. Why burn a rare exploit when a convincing form will do?

Osservatorio Nessuno blames weak Italian and European rules, opacity and missing ethical limits. Civil liberties coalition Cild wants to know why authorities would target asylum seekers, who authorized it and what it produced.

IPS has plenty of company, including RCS, Area, SIO and Innova. Nine established firms reported €214.4 million in 2024 revenue from surveillance and intelligence tools. Italy supplied 72.8% of revenue among the five disclosing geography.

Spyware targets rose from 2,894 in 2021 to 5,611 in 2025, up about 94%. Mafia cases accounted for 2,930 last year, ordinary crimes for 2,618 and terrorism for 63. Germany authorized 91 spyware operations in 2024 and carried out 57.

Italian law permits spyware well beyond mafia and terrorism cases, including corruption and many ordinary crimes. Defence lawyer Eugenio Losco warns that a criminal association charge opens the door, even against unionists and activists. Interception spending also reached about €299 million in 2025, despite a national price list meant to cut costs.

Controls remain thin. Italy has no prior quality check, and authorities lack the skills to verify the software. In the eSurv case, a company kept intercepts on its own servers, and its trial continues.

Magistrates’ association vice president Marcello De Chiara calls spyware essential and current safeguards for its use adequate. He wants tighter rules for private vendors, including a national register and clearer ownership.

The concern extends beyond this app. A researcher says at least five activists recently received spyware links, while Sea-Watch’s Giorgia Linardi has reported zero-click surveillance in 2021. IrpiMedia doesn’t tie either case to FirmaDigitale.

Lawyer Giovanni Battista Gallus says Italy lacks a complete law limiting what spyware can do. Collected data can remain archived, and authorities needn’t notify targets when an investigation is shelved. IrpiMedia captures the result, that targets may never find out, in one sentence:

“Those who are spied upon may never even find out. In fact, there is no obligation to notify an individual that they have been subjected to surveillance once an investigation is closed.” Gallus concludes. “Furthermore, while the surveillance tools must be deactivated at the end of an operation, the material collected is not automatically deleted; instead, it may remain stored in the public prosecutor’s archives.”

[15] Security Affairs - Air Force Cloud One Breach: Updates Blocked, Migration Paused, Scope Unknown
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Fri, 27 Nov 2026 15:03:05 +0000
Link: https://securityaffairs.com/204136/security/air-force-cloud-one-breach-updates-blocked-migration-paused-scope-unknown.html

[TAM METİN - 620 kelime]
Air Force Cloud One Breach: Updates Blocked, Migration Paused, Scope Unknown

Reuters reports a breach of the US Air Force’s Cloud One. The scope, attackers and data stolen remain unknown; updates are blocked.

Reuters reports that hackers have breached Cloud One, one of the US Air Force ‘s main cloud computing platforms. The breach could put a large amount of sensitive military data at risk, including information about military operations, personnel, and logistics.

Two sources said the intrusion was discovered about a month ago during work to upgrade the platform. The discovery has disrupted the entire service. Reuters could not identify the attackers, determine how much of the platform was affected, or confirm whether any data had been stolen.

“Hackers have breached the US Air Force’s flagship cloud computing platform, three people familiar with the matter told ​Reuters, in a development that could jeopardize a vast swathe of sensitive tactical, personnel and logistics data.” Reuters states.

“does not comment on the ​security posture, vulnerabilities or operational status of specific networks and systems.” reads a statement sent to Reuters by the Air Force. “We continuously take prudent measures to protect Department (of the Air Force) information, systems ​and mission capabilities.”

Cloud One started in 2017 as a one-stop shop for the Air Force’s IT needs. It combines the resources of four operators, Microsoft, Amazon, Oracle and Alphabet, to run scores of applications, including ones for personnel data, pay, logistics and maintenance. Two of the sources say it’s among the military’s biggest cloud platforms.

Two applications show why the breach is serious. Cloud-Based Command and Control brings together air defense radar data and other information from across North America. The Theater Integrated Combat Munitions System tracks bombs and missiles at more than 200 US Air Force locations worldwide.

SAIC was the main contractor until 2025, when Leidos took over. SAIC did not respond to Reuters, while Leidos referred questions to the Air Force. According to one source, investigators have asked US Cyber Command, the military’s cyber unit, to assess the damage. Cyber Command did not respond to Reuters either.

“A breach here is a big deal,” one of the sources told Reuters.

The disruption is already visible. Two sources, plus a fourth person familiar with the matter, say efforts to migrate back-office applications to Cloud One, a longstanding military priority, have been paused. A fifth source says some users had their administrator rights revoked.

The timing makes the incident more serious. Updates to applications running on Cloud One were blocked, leaving Air Force personnel unable to install fixes. This happened just as several serious software vulnerabilities were being disclosed across the technology industry. All five sources spoke to Reuters on condition of anonymity.

According to one source, the attackers exploited a software flaw to access the platform’s control plane, which manages its main functions. The source compared it to a universal remote control.

“Updates to applications already running on Cloud One were blocked, leaving Air Force personnel unable to apply fixes just as a new batch of serious software vulnerabilities has been disclosed across the technology industry.” Reuters continues. “A fifth source said that some users had their administrator rights revoked.”

The breach lands amid heightened anxiety over AI-powered intrusions. Cloud networks are a particular worry because AI models can swiftly detect weaknesses across wide surface areas. The US government is already dealing with at least six other recently disclosed incidents, including two at the FBI, a previous hack of Pentagon data, a leak of Department of Homeland Security personnel information, a breach affecting at least two spy agencies and a hack at the Treasury Department.

[16] Security Affairs - SAP Security Incident: Stolen Tokens Raise Supply Chain Concerns
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Fri, 27 Nov 2026 11:39:20 +0000
Link: https://securityaffairs.com/204121/data-breach/sap-security-incident-stolen-tokens-raise-supply-chain-concerns.html

[TAM METİN - 375 kelime]
SAP Security Incident: Stolen Tokens Raise Supply Chain Concerns

SAP confirms a security incident as reports point to stolen GitHub tokens and altered source code. Data breach remains unconfirmed.

SAP has confirmed a security incident, and most of the details come from the press, not from the company. The global enterprise found suspicious activity in its IT systems. DER SPIEGEL reported that attackers stole company employees’ access tokens and used them to change and copy source code on GitHub.

“According to information obtained by SPIEGEL, the software manufacturer SAP is now dealing with an incident. The perpetrators may have used AI systems for assistance.” reads the report published by DER SPIEGEL.

The German software maker says it took immediate steps to protect customers and secure its services.

SAP says investigators had found no evidence of unauthorized access to customer business data at that stage. It didn’t answer detailed questions about the incident. An internal memo cited by Spiegel calls the incident active, and SAP is reportedly handling the investigation under the name “Project Granite”.

Sources inside the software giant also told Spiegel that the attackers may have used AI agents. SAP has not confirmed this, and it is still unclear whether AI played any role in the attack. For now, this should be treated as an unconfirmed report.

Employees were reportedly told not to discuss the incident. The report also says SAP management does not believe it needs to inform customers proactively. SAP reportedly told German broadcaster Tagesschau that customer data was not affected.

SAP has not disclosed how the attackers obtained the employees’ tokens. Cybersecurity News recently reported a separate case in which a malicious VS Code extension stole GitHub tokens, but there is no evidence linking that attack to the SAP incident.

SAP publishes source code for its products and open-source projects on GitHub. However, the report does not say which repositories were affected or whether the stolen code was public or private. This information is important to understand the impact of the incident.

GitHub’s audit log guidance explains how organizations can trace recorded actions back to a compromised token, and administrators can revoke credentials by user and token type.

[17] Security Affairs - FIFA Confirms a Cyberattack, Hackers Claim 12 GB of Internal Files
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Fri, 27 Nov 2026 08:48:03 +0000
Link: https://securityaffairs.com/204100/data-breach/fifa-confirms-a-cyberattack-hackers-claim-12-gb-of-internal-files.html

[TAM METİN - 503 kelime]
FIFA Confirms a Cyberattack, Hackers Claim 12 GB of Internal Files

FIFA confirms a cyberattack exposing personal and business data, while the Handsome Boy Hacking Club claims responsibility for the breach.

FIFA confirmed it was the victim of a cyberattack, threat actors gained access to what soccer’s world governing body describes as a “limited set of personal and business information.” The organization told The Associated Press it’s working closely with the relevant authorities, including law enforcement.

FIFA did not disclose technical details about the attack or what kind of information hackers accessed.

A hacking group, which calls itself the Handsome Boy Hacking Club, claimed the security incident before FIFA made its announcement. The threat actors shared a screenshot on X showing what it claimed were FIFA files. Three days later, the group appeared to claim responsibility for the attack in a reply to a post reporting that more than 12 GB of FIFA internal documents had been leaked.

‼️ FIFA has confirmed that hackers broke into its systems and accessed personal and business information.A new group calling itself Handsome Boy Hacking Club claims it took more than 12GB from FIFA's Microsoft environment, including identity and immigration documents, and… pic.twitter.com/ZwxJ7LoQez

At this moment, the group’s X account has been suspended, and their Tor leak site is unreachable.

“FIFA condemns in the strongest possible terms the unlawful acquisition and publication of information, particularly personal data,” states the organization. “Such actions constitute a serious and unacceptable violation of privacy and can have significant consequences for the individuals concerned.”

The breach comes at a sensitive time for FIFA and its president, Gianni Infantino, who faces criticism over a failed plan to sell World Cup stakes to private investors and his bid to extend his presidency to 2031.

He remains the favorite ahead of the March 18 election, against Kohzo Tashima and Philippe Diallo. The AP report does not link the cyberattack to these issues. FIFA warns that the exposure of personal data could have serious consequences for those affected.

“Such actions constitute a serious and unacceptable violation of privacy and can have significant consequences for the individuals concerned.” concludes the AP.

Handsome Boy Hacking Club (HBHC) is a newly emerged ransomware and data-extortion group first observed in November 2026. Threat intelligence platforms recorded its first activity on November 19, when it listed three organizations on its data-leak site: US-based firearms manufacturer Smith & Wesson, healthcare provider UCHealth, and telecommunications company Frontier Communications. On November 20, the group added FIFA to its victim list, bringing the number of publicly recorded claims to four.

The group has provided little information about its operators, origins, or technical capabilities. Its claims involve organizations in the United States and Switzerland, spanning the manufacturing, healthcare, telecommunications, and sports sectors. While FIFA has confirmed a cyberattack resulting in unauthorized access to personal and business information, the other listings should be treated as claims that have not necessarily been independently verified.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Fri, 27 Nov 2026 11:01:00 +0100
Link: https://www.theregister.com/security/2026/11/27/british-social-security-system-techies-yank-services-offline-following-cyber-incident/5312522

British social security system techies yank services offline following 'cyber incident'

Pensioners assured their funds will arrive as normal

Unkernel and Trynix just transported computing to 2035
now

Smartphone shipments face another bleak year before modest 2028 comeback
17 hours ago

Bill for Birmingham's Oracle debacle reaches £128M
18 hours ago

UK government rejects national consultation on AI datacenters
19 hours ago

Lloyds and Bank of Scotland apps fall over on payday
20 hours ago

The UK's Department for Work and Pensions (DWP) has taken some services offline after detecting a "cyber incident," but says regular benefit and pension payments are unaffected.

The department announced the precautionary shutdown on Friday morning. "We have taken some DWP online services offline as a precaution after we identified a cyber incident affecting our systems," it said.

"We are working closely with the National Cyber Security Centre and Government Cyber Coordination Centre (GC3) to investigate and to bring services back online as quickly as possible and to minimize any disruption.

"Regular benefit and pension payments are not affected and will be paid as normal. If an online service you need is unavailable, please try again later. As always, we will never message you to ask for bank details or ask you to click on links."

The Register contacted both the DWP and NCSC for more information. The DWP declined to comment beyond the statement issued publicly.

GC3 coordinates responses to incidents affecting government. The Government Cyber Unit leads the wider Government Cyber Action Plan, backed by more than £210 million. It is led by the government CISO, Bella Powell.

Officials have not commented on any of the usual details one would expect from a cyber incident disclosure, including the specific services that were downed, when they are expected to return, or whether any data is thought to be compromised, even at this early stage.

The UK government has also been criticized recently for investing heavily in AI cyber defenses, while the issue of legacy and aging IT continues to be neglected in the background.

It announced at the end of October that it was throwing £130 million at AI cyber defense technology, as part of a wider £600 million cross-government AI spending spree announced by the Treasury.

ExfilSquad recently claimed to have stolen around 607,000 records from the Department for Education and another 114,000 from the Police National Legal Database. ®

[19] CSO Online - Flaw in Linux NFS file sharing exposes servers to remote takeover
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Fri, 27 Nov 2026 20:43:35 +0000
Link: https://www.csoonline.com/article/4254114/flaw-in-linux-nfs-file-sharing-exposes-servers-to-remote-takeover.html

[TAM METİN - 368 kelime]
The vulnerability went undetected in the Linux kernel for 12 years and allows anyone who reaches a server's file-sharing port to gain root access without a password.

Researchers have found a critical flaw in the Linux kernel’s NFS file-sharing server that lets unauthenticated attackers obtain a root shell on affected systems. The flaw remained undetected in the kernel for 12 years, until two researchers, one using an AI agent, discovered it independently.

NFS is typically used inside local networks, including in container networks in Kubernetes clusters and on build servers in development environments. However, it is also enabled on many embedded devices and in network-attached storage (NAS) devices which might be running older kernel versions and can only be patched with firmware updates.

Tracked as CVE-2026-99362 and dubbed NFS2ROOT, the flaw affects systems running the NFSv4.1 or NFSv4.2 versions of the Network File System protocol, the default in many Linux distributions. The vulnerability allows anyone who can reach a server’s port 2049 to corrupt the memory of the nfsd process, the kernel component that serves NFS requests. It is rated 9.8 in severity.

A researcher with French security agency ANSSI, Jérémy Jean, found the bug in August, and his fix was merged into the mainline kernel on September 14.

According to security firm OpenSec Intelligence, which also discovered the heap overflow vulnerability and published technical details about it this week, the flaw can be exploited to gain a shell with root privileges. OpenSec found the bug using its AI-powered security testing harness and submitted its own patch to the Linux kernel maintainers on September 22.

However, since it takes time for all supported kernel branches to backport fixes, the bug wasn’t disclosed, nor was a CVE ID assigned, until November 19.

The flaw was introduced in Linux 3.16 in 2014 and is now patched in eight stable kernel branches: 5.10.275, 5.15.226, 6.1.193, 6.6.163, 6.12.117, 6.18.61, 7.3.2 and 7.4-rc1.

On systems that can’t be immediately patched, administrators are advised to use firewall rules that block access to port 2049 from untrusted systems. Another option is to turn off NFSv4.1 and 4.2 in the nfsd config and then restart the service, but this will turn off pNFS and some features.

(XXXXXXX, AÇIK - https://www.csoonline.com/article/4254114/flaw-in-linux-nfs-file-sharing-exposes-servers-to-remote-takeover.html, csoonline.com, 27.11.2026)
